## 2026-10-23T03:23:52Z — ORCHESTRATOR DECISION for V2 (unblocks blocked:orchestrator)
RULING D12 (2026-10-23T03:23:52Z, orchestrator; answers agent-20261023T0151-P1-unbound-anchor and app2-20261023T0157Z-p1-legacy-timing-policy): **final validation refuses only new invalidity.**

A commit is refused (422 invalid) only if it:
- creates an unresolvable timing or reference declaration;
- modifies one; or
- makes a previously resolvable one unresolvable, for example by removing or moving its anchor target.

An unresolvable legacy declaration already in the base, which the commit doesn't touch and whose referenced targets it doesn't change, is preserved byte-for-byte and doesn't block unrelated edits. A dry run or validate may report it as a non-blocking diagnostic.

Why: existing documents must stay editable (upstream behaviour). The four original P1 positives encode exactly that. Inventing bindings, stripping declarations or turning positives into refusals are all ruled out. This does not weaken the timing guard, which still applies in full to anything the commit writes or invalidates.

Tests:
- keep the four original positives;
- add negatives for create, modify and invalidate-by-target-change of an unresolved declaration.
Agent: implement this in V2 now, with a Luna repair on e24f91385 (Sol only if it fails), keeping every existing guard and assertion. Check: V2 shows doing, with a worker, and the four P1 fixtures pass with the new negatives added.

RULING D13 (2026-10-23T03:40:13Z; answers app2-20261023T0335Z-p1-atomic-merge-seam): **App gesture saves declare the reads their gesture used, and send merge=fields.**

The gesture handler knows its intent, so it states its reads:
- the edited items' fields it consulted (for a trim, that clip's timing fields);
- the parent collection's membership and order only when the gesture depends on them (for a split, ripple or reorder, yes; for a trim, no).

Pass them through timelineLocalSaveHandoff → documentPatchIntent → localSaveSession as contract reads, with merge=fields.

Unchanged:
- saves with no known intent (free or unknown transforms, imports) stay atomic, exactly as now;
- reads are never derived from byte diffs and never invented as reads:[];
- the atomic guard is never weakened, and there is no second executor.

This is A2 scope (PLAN §3: merged replies are part of the App chain), not new scope.

Tests:
- an intervening Agent edit to an unrelated field or label on the same parent → the trim MERGES;
- an intervening edit to the trimmed clip's own timing → refused, as a conflict;
- existing atomic cases are unchanged.

A consistency rule such as fps parity between config.output.fps and canvas.fps fires only when the commit writes one of the fields involved. Then both must agree, using the existing exact normalizers.

An untouched legacy mismatch (29.97002997002997 vs 30000/1001) is preserved byte-for-byte and doesn't block unrelated edits (initial track.create, anchor-source changes). A dry run may show it as a diagnostic.

- a limit_denominator;
- an invented equivalence;
- rewriting the fixture.

The same goes for the untouched legacy elementRef without an id.

Tests:
- keep the originals;
- add negatives: a commit that writes one fps field to a value that disagrees is refused; one that writes both consistently is accepted.

No further rulings are needed for other D12 cases. Apply it yourselves.

## 2026-10-23T06:22:10Z — ORCHESTRATOR DECISION D16 (for information)
RULING D16 (2026-10-23T06:22:10Z; answers viewing-20261023T0549-stale-window-positive-vs-invalidation): **this is D12, so it isn't deferred.**

Moving fixture.window from 4..6 so that it excludes the retained word (3..3.5) makes a resolved declaration unresolvable. Under D12 that edit is itself refused (422 invalid, with the anchor reference path). That is the stricter, intended v2 behaviour, and the guard stays exactly as it is.

What the test protects still holds: a stale anchor never writes silently, and the refusal carries the reason, the head, no write, and the word and source facts. The refusal now happens earlier, at the window edit.

Viewing:
- adapt this one test's expectation to the earlier refusal point (a bounded expectation adaptation, like your four status rows);
- keep every original no-write, word and source assertion, and add the reference-path assertion;
- get an independent review that confirms no assertion is weakened;
- list it in the delivery as a behaviour change ('source-window edits that orphan an anchor are refused at edit time').

Not allowed: a guard change, a C0 change, invented equivalence, or a skip. **Principle:** when v2 refuses earlier than v1 for the same protected property, adapt the test to the earlier refusal and keep all its assertions. D14-style deferral is only for behaviour v2 can't provide at all.

## 2026-10-23T08:39:24Z — ORCHESTRATOR DECISION D17
RULING D17 (2026-10-23T08:39:24Z; answers agent-20261023T0816-G5-duplicate-authority): **cross-timeline duplicate is IN scope.** It is a writer migration, and PLAN §3 says never cut writer migrations or tests of promised behaviour.

Build it as **one semantic provider command under the existing semantic-command carrier.** Prefer no schema change, so the 34eac freeze holds; if the schema truly can't carry it, it's bundled once, per the freeze rule.
- **Agent (Sol):** an authenticated source-copy duplicate. It copies an exact source timeline, revision or publication into fresh identities, preserves repeated sharing, and supports undo and idempotency.
- **Runtime (Luna, with Sol if it fails):** the authority. Runtime verifies the source pair and digest, the caller's access to the source, and the media inputs, inside the existing provider-execution path. No Runtime-only executor, and no guard change.

**Every negative listed in the escalation is mandatory:** forged media, foreign source, outside source, stale source, occupied ID, order, sharing, undo, idempotency and archive cleanup.

**G5:** merge every other deletion as soon as P1 is GO. The old duplicate writer is deleted only once the new command passes both original duplicate tests. Until then that one old path stays, listed as remaining on the board.

## 2026-10-23T12:46:12Z — ORCHESTRATOR
RULING D21 (2026-10-23T12:46:12Z; separate harness qualification from the proof):
1. **Provision benches through the dispatcher, never by hand.** For each gate tuple (P1, P2, P3, and full suites), request one immutable bench, using:
   - `bin/request-child-venv` for the Agent child venv at the exact SHA;
   - `bin/request-runtime` for the Remotion runtime;
   - `bin/request-checkout` for exact pins.

   Neither needs an escalation. No worker hand-links venvs or builds environments inside its sandbox.
2. **Report-all preflight first.** Every gate run starts with a preflight on the real runner, run with a trivial boot: the prelude, the beforeAll, the venv, the runtime, the snapshot identity and the seed. It reports EVERY failure in one pass, not just the first.
3. **Harness-only changes need no independent review.** That means a change that touches only the suite row, prelude, bench or environment, with the original test BODY byte-identical. Prove that with a body hash recorded in the run, the same pattern as conflict-free merge-forwards. They go straight to the run. A body change still needs the full independent no-weakening review.

This doesn't weaken the proof: the body is hash-locked.

UPSTREAM RULE (2026-10-23T12:46:12Z): **defer by default.** Record each new upstream tip in the drift line on the BOARD-HEADER, and re-preview every 3 h.
- **Merge early only if** the change touches gate paths (contract, schemas, generated clients, commit/store, outbox/save path, provider, test support, locks, pins), fixes a failure we're hitting, or is a security fix.
- **Never merge upstream into a repo while an exact-pin proof on it is pending.** Queue it behind that GO.
- **Otherwise:** one U-task per repo at the final freeze, then a gate re-run.

Now: App 6e3445ee9 (tests/CI) waits until after P1 GO; Runtime 6162e0e33 (tests only) waits for the freeze. U2b (Agent, already in review) may finish, since it's in flight.
